{"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"text":"Report written to `/results/encodings.json`, and the server on `127.0.0.1:6379` is up and serving `hash` as `listpack` from a private copy of `hash-ziplist.rdb`.\n\n## Results\n\n| file | key | type | default | tight |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| hash-zipmap.rdb | hash | hash | listpack | hashtable |\n| hash-ziplist.rdb | hash | hash | listpack | hashtable |\n| zset-ziplist.rdb | zset | zset | listpack | skiplist |\n| list-quicklist.rdb | list | list | listpack | **listpack** |\n\nPayloads: both hashes hold `f1→v1`, `f2→v2`; the zset holds `one→1`, `two→2`; the list holds a single element `\"7\"`.\n\n## One result that won't match the brief's expectation\n\n`list-max-listpack-size 1` does not force the list off a compact encoding, so `tight_encoding` is `listpack`, same as the default. This is real loader behavior, not a config that failed to apply — I verified the limit was live at 7199 (`config get list-max-listpack-size` → `1`), and an `RPUSH` of a second element flipped the key to `quicklist` immediately.\n\nThe reason is in `src/quicklist.c:467`: `quicklistNodeLimit` clamps a positive `fill` to `*count = (fill == 0) ? 1 : fill`, so the smallest reachable count limit is 1. After loading, `listTypeTryConversion(o, LIST_CONV_AUTO, ...)` (`src/rdb.c:2237`) calls `listTypeTryConvertQuicklist`, which converts a single-node quicklist down to listpack when `ql->count > count_limit` is false (`src/t_list.c:104`). The dump holds exactly one element, so `1 > 1` is false and it becomes a listpack. No positive `list-max-listpack-size` can force quicklist for a one-element list; that would need a count limit of 0, which the config cannot express. The hash and zset cases behave as expected because their dumps hold two entries against a limit of 1.\n\nI recorded the observed value rather than the expected one. If ops needs this snapshot to land as a quicklist, the lever is a byte-size limit (negative `list-max-listpack-size`, e.g. `-1` = 4 KB) rather than a count — though a 13-byte listpack is well under even that, so a one-element list will stay compact in practice.\n\n## Method and side effects\n\nEach of the eight loads (four files × two modes) ran on its own port (7101-7108) with `bind 127.0.0.1`, no password, and a private `dir` containing only a copy of that one dump.
